description: Open PR and watch CI
command: cd "C:\Users\avidu\Projects\sports-data-collector"; gh pr create --title "feat(curate): preflight gate for prep-annotation-batch (duplicate/empty/VFR checks)" --body @'
## What

Bakes the manual reconnaissance checks that caught two real issues on the 2026-06-15 Roora batch into the tool, so they **gate** the 1080p proxy creation instead of relying on someone running probes by hand.

New `src/core/batch_preflight.py` (pure, offline-testable) — discovers the input tree one level deep, header-only-probes every video, and reports:
- **DUPLICATE videos** (same content in >1 folder) via a fast `size + head-MB + tail-MB` fingerprint that never full-hashes a 10 GB file over USB → **BLOCKER**;
- **empty subdirs**, **variable-frame-rate** sources, **multi-video** folders → surfaced warnings;
- a per-video resolution / fps / VFR table.

`prep-annotation-batch` now runs the preflight **first** and refuses to start on a duplicate unless `--allow-duplicates` (for an intentional A/A annotation-consistency test); `--dry-run` prints the report and stops without encoding. The batch reuses the preflight's already-probed entries.

## The two issues this would have caught automatically (it did, by hand)
- `Video 2 - Tabletennis` was an **empty folder** (forgotten clip).
- `Video 6` and `Video 9` were the **byte-identical** `GX010132.MP4` (a copy, not two matches).

## Performance
Added `proxy.probe_light` (header-only, **no `-count_packets`**) so the scan is seconds across a whole USB tree rather than reading all 115 GB. `probe_video` still does the exact packet count where per-video parity actually needs it. **Live dry-run on the real 12-video / 115 GB June-15 tree: 3.8 s, "All clear ✅".**

## Verification
- `tests/test_batch_preflight.py` (14 tests: fingerprint identity/size-sensitivity, discovery + empty dirs, duplicate/VFR/multi-dir/probe-error detection, and the CLI gate — blocks on dup, `--dry-run` writes nothing, `--allow-duplicates` proceeds) + a `probe_light` test asserting no packet counting.
- **Suite 195 green; ruff clean.**
- Docs: `ROORA_RUNBOOK` + `INGESTION_PIPELINE` document the gate and the `--dry-run` / `--allow-duplicates` flags.

214	    return abs(info.r_fps - info.avg_fps) / info.avg_fps > rel_tol

216	
217	def build_proxy_command(src: str, dst: str, height: int = DEFAULT_HEIGHT,
218	                        crf: int = DEFAULT_CRF, gop: int = DEFAULT_GOP,
219	                        preset: str = DEFAULT_PRESET) -> List[str]:
220	    """The ffmpeg invocation that downscales WITHOUT touching the timeline.
221	
222	    Invariants (ADR-002): no ``-r`` / fps filter (fps must never change), no
223	    trim, ``-fps_mode passthrough`` (ffmpeg >= 5.1) so every source frame maps
224	    1:1 onto a proxy frame with its original timestamp. ``min(height, ih)``
225	    never upscales. Audio is kept (contact sound is the annotator's best
226	    boundary cue).
227	    """
228	    return [
229	        "ffmpeg", "-hide_banner", "-loglevel", "warning", "-stats", "-y",
230	        "-i", src,
231	        # 0:V:0 = the first REAL video stream (capital V excludes cover art).
232	        "-map", "0:V:0", "-map", "0:a?",
233	        # 2*trunc(.../2) keeps the height even for yuv420p when an odd-height
234	        # source slips under the cap (a 1 px change; the timeline is untouched).
235	        "-vf", f"scale=-2:2*trunc(min({height}\\,ih)/2)",
236	        "-c:v", "libx264", "-preset", preset, "-crf", str(crf),
237	        "-g", str(gop), "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p",
238	        "-fps_mode", "passthrough",
239	        "-c:a", "aac", "-b:a", "160k",
240	        "-movflags", "+faststart",
241	        dst,
242	    ]
243	
244	
245	def run_ffmpeg(cmd: List[str]) -> None:
246	    """Run an ffmpeg command, streaming its progress to the console."""
247	    if not shutil.which("ffmpeg"):
248	        raise ProxyError("ffmpeg not found on PATH (install FFmpeg)")
249	    try:
250	        # No capture: -stats progress and any warnings go straight to the user.
251	        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True)
252	    except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError) as e:
253	        code = getattr(e, "returncode", "?")
254	        raise ProxyError(f"ffmpeg failed (exit {code}) — proxy not produced")
255

646	    def prep_annotation_proxy(self, args):
647	        """Re-encode a video into a frame-identical annotation proxy (ADR-002).
648	
649	        The proxy is what the annotation vendor receives: downscaled and
650	        scrub-friendly (dense keyframes) so CVAT frame-step-1 work is cheap,
651	        but with the source's EXACT frame count / fps / timeline, so frame N
652	        on the proxy is frame N on the original and returned labels apply to
653	        the original unchanged. Parity is verified after the encode; a proxy
654	        that fails verification is deleted, never shared. Provenance (source +
655	        proxy MD5s) is appended to the proxy manifest — downstream processing
656	        (import-local, export, the indexer) keeps running on the ORIGINAL file.
657	        """
658	        from src.core import proxy as proxylib
659	
660	        src = args.video_path

662	            print(f"Error: video '{src}' not found.")
663	            sys.exit(1)

665	        stem = os.path.basename(src).split('.')[0]
666	        # Mirror import-local's video_id derivation so the manifest row and a
667	        # later DB registration of the same source agree on the key.

673	                     == os.path.normcase(os.path.realpath(src)))

- An availability guard fails loudly if `--encoder nvenc` is requested on a box without `h264_nvenc` (rather than silently falling back to a slow CPU encode).
- 201 tests green, new code ruff-clean.

description: Open the NVENC PR
command: cd "C:\Users\avidu\Projects\sports-data-collector"; gh pr create --title "feat(curate): --encoder nvenc (GPU) for proxy + batch transcode" --body @'
## What

Adds an NVIDIA GPU encode path to the proxy tooling so 4K→1080p batches don''t burn the CPU — a laptop-safe concern now and the right default for Phase B''s 36-45 videos. `--encoder {libx264,nvenc}`, default `libx264` (nothing changes for existing callers).

- `proxy.build_proxy_command(encoder=...)`: the `nvenc` branch uses `h264_nvenc` with constant-quality VBR (`-rc vbr -cq <crf> -b:v 0`) at preset `p5`; the libx264 path is byte-for-byte unchanged. The ADR-002 timeline invariants are identical for both (no `-r`, no trim, `-fps_mode passthrough`, even-height scale), so **parity is codec-agnostic** — NVENC output is verified exactly like CPU output.
- `proxy.encoder_available()`: a deliberate `--encoder nvenc` on a box without `h264_nvenc` errors loudly rather than silently falling back to a slow CPU encode.
- `curate`: `--encoder` on both `prep-annotation-proxy` and `prep-annotation-batch` (passed through per-video); availability checked before the encode; the manifest''s `encoder_settings` records which encoder ran.

## Verification

- Suite **201 green**, new code ruff-clean.
- **Live NVENC litmus** on a real 8.8 GB 4K/59.94 GoPro clip (RTX 2070 Super): **parity VERIFIED — 36,901 frames @ 59.94 on both**. cq19 proxy = 2949 MB (~2× libx264 medium, ~3× smaller than source); wall time was I/O-bound (USB read ×2 + Drive write) with the CPU idle — exactly the laptop-safe behaviour intended.

## Notes

Raise `--crf` (→ `-cq`, e.g. 21-23) to shrink NVENC files if Drive upload is slow. Docs: ROORA_RUNBOOK gains a CPU-vs-GPU encoder section.

1	# Roora Annotation Brief — Rally Golden Set
2	
3	**Project:** racket-sport rally segmentation golden / regression dataset
4	**Sports in scope:** Badminton, Pickleball, Table Tennis (each in **singles _and_ doubles**)
5	**Companion doc:** [`ANNOTATION_GUIDE.md`](./ANNOTATION_GUIDE.md)
6	
7	> This brief is the vendor-facing spec. The annotation deliverable is a per-rally CSV that
8	> imports directly via `python -m src.cli.curate import-local`.
9	
10	## 0. What we need in one sentence
11	For each provided match video, produce **one CSV file** listing **every rally** with its
12	start time, end time, number of shots, and how it ended — **excluding all dead time**.
13	
14	## 1. Deliverable format (canonical)
15	One CSV per video (UTF-8), named exactly `<video_id>.csv` (we supply the `video_id`).
16	One row per rally, chronological, header row required.
17	
18	| column | meaning |
19	|---|---|
20	| `rally_number` | integer, 1-based, chronological. No gaps, no repeats. |
21	| `start_mmss` | rater-entered time as `m:ss.mmm` (e.g. `1:12.40`) — serve contact. |
22	| `end_mmss` | rater-entered time as `m:ss.mmm` — ball/shuttle dead. |
23	| `start_time` | decimal **seconds** (e.g. `72.40`). **Auto-filled** from `start_mmss`. |
24	| `end_time` | decimal **seconds**. **Auto-filled** from `end_mmss`. |
25	| `shots_count` | integer. Total racket/paddle/bat contacts (serve = shot 1). |
26	| `ending_reason` | one of: `winner` \| `forced_error` \| `unforced_error` \| `service_fault` \| `let` \| `other` (**why** it ended) |
27	| `last_shot_outcome` | one of: `in` \| `net` \| `out` \| `n_a` (**where** the last shot landed; `n_a` for service_fault/let) |
28	| `score_before` | optional, `"A-B"`. Blank if not confidently readable. |
29	| `score_after` | optional, `"A-B"`. |
30	| `notes` | optional free text. **Required** when `ending_reason = other`. |
31	
32	**Times (mm:ss → decimal seconds).** Raters type only `start_mmss`/`end_mmss`. The template
33	Google Sheet fills `start_time`/`end_time` with this formula (shown for `start_time`, row 2):
34	
35	```
36	=IF(B2="","", IFERROR(VALUE(LEFT(B2,FIND(":",B2)-1))*60 + VALUE(MID(B2,FIND(":",B2)+1,20)), VALUE(B2)))
37	```
38	
39	On **Download as CSV**, the computed decimal seconds are exported. (If your tool already
40	shows decimal seconds, type those into `start_time`/`end_time` and leave the mm:ss columns blank.)
41	
42	Example (badminton):
43	```csv
44	rally_number,start_mmss,end_mmss,start_time,end_time,shots_count,ending_reason,last_shot_outcome,score_before,score_after,notes
45	1,0:12.50,0:25.00,12.50,25.00,9,winner,in,0-0,1-0,
46	2,0:45.00,0:47.30,45.00,47.30,1,service_fault,n_a,1-0,1-1,serve into net
47	3,1:01.20,1:18.85,61.20,78.85,14,unforced_error,out,1-1,2-1,final clear sailed long (no pressure)
48	```
49	
50	**Critical format rules**
51	- `start_time`/`end_time` that reach us **must be decimal seconds** (the formula guarantees this).
52	- Times are relative to the **start of the file we provide**. Do **not** trim, clip, or
53	  re-encode the video — our pipeline identifies a video by its file checksum. (The file we
54	  share may be an **annotation-optimized copy** of the original recording — lighter and
55	  faster to frame-step, but frame-for-frame identical to it. Treat it exactly like the
56	  original: annotate it as-is.)
57	- `end_time` > `start_time` for every row.
58	- No overlaps: `end_time[N] <= start_time[N+1]`.
59	- Everything between one rally's `end_time` and the next rally's `start_time` is **dead time** and gets **no row**.
60	
61	## 2. The three non-negotiable rules (full detail + pictures in the Guide)
62	1. **Ignore all dead time.** Only label active play.
63	2. **Include every rally — exclude none.** Every served point, including **service faults**,
64	   1–2 shot rallies, and **lets/replays**. A missed rally is the worst error in this project.
65	   **Exception: warm-up / knock-up is NOT a rally** — pre-match and between-games warm-up looks
66	   like rallying but ...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

### 856. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.140Z)

Batch is **live on the GPU** — currently encoding at ~65 fps / ~1.1× realtime (GPU keeping up; USB read is the limiter, CPU idle). It'll grind through the 11 remaining videos over your lunch. I'll report the full summary when it lands. Now to the Roora guidelines.

First, a mechanics note: I can **read** that Google Doc but the Drive tools I have don't let me **edit it in place** (no update-doc tool). That's fine, because the Doc's own header says it's *generated from the repo* — `ROORA_BRIEF.md` + `ANNOTATION_GUIDE.md` are the source of truth. So I'll propose changes there, and once you pick scope I'll implement them + hand you paste-ready text to regenerate the Doc.

## Exactly what we ask Roora today

**The deliverable — one CSV per video, one row per rally, these 11 fields:**

| Field | What it captures | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `rally_number` | 1-based sequence | no gaps/repeats |
| `start_mmss` / `start_time` | serve-contact time | mm:ss typed → seconds auto-filled |
| `end_mmss` / `end_time` | ball/shuttle-dead time | ±0.3 s bar; must hit exact frames |
| `shots_count` | total racket contacts | serve = shot 1 |
| `ending_reason` | *why* it ended | winner / forced_error / unforced_error / service_fault / let / other |
| `last_shot_outcome` | *where* last shot landed | in / net / out / n_a |
| `score_before` / `score_after` | scoreboard | optional, blank if unreadable |
| `notes` | free text | required when `other` |

**Plus the rules** (ignore dead time; label every rally incl. faults/lets but not warm-up; skip rallies running at file edges; shot = one contact) and **the asks in §8–9** (pricing per rally/minute, turnaround, throughput, QA/rework policy, racket-sport familiarity).

So today we capture **timing + count + outcome taxonomy + score**. That's solid for *rally segmentation*, but it leaves real signal on the table — especially given what you're paying for and what the model is weak at.

## My feedback — what richer info to buy now that it's paid

The single highest-value gap maps directly to your own corpus design. You deliberately stacked **multi-court badminton** videos because that's where the model collapses (**0.66 vs 0.93** on clean footage). But our current schema gives Roora **no way to tell us which court a rally is on** — so we get rally times with no signal about the exact confound we most need to beat. Fixing that is the headline.

Prioritized, with value vs. cost (each new field is one more CVAT dropdown for them + a small additive change in our `convert-cvat → CSV → DB` path):

1. **🟢 Court tag — `court = primary | secondary`** *(highest value)*. Per rally, is it on the main court the camera is filming, or a background court? This is the gold label for the multi-court problem: it lets us train the model to suppress background-court pickups (its #1 false-positive source) and measure accuracy per court. Consistent across videos (binary, not arbitrary court numbers). Low annotator burden, high payoff.
2. **🟢 Per-rally `confidence` / `review` flag** *(cheap, high value)*. A simple `sure | unsure` per rally. Lets us down-weight ambiguous labels in training and — crucially — tells us *where our own guidelines are unclear*, which sharpens the next iteration. Nearly free for them.
3. **🟡 Per-video capture-quality report** *(feeds the product loop)*. A short structured note per video: occlusion, glare/lighting, whether the whole court is in frame, camera shake. This both flags inputs that will hurt the model and directly improves our `VIDEO_RECORDING_GUIDELINES`. One short form per video, not per rally.
4. **🟡 Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) overlap** *(process, not a field)*. Pay **two** raters on a ~15% overlap sample and measure consistency — this is exactly your A/A idea, and now that it's paid you can afford it. It gives a measured quality floor and catches systematic rater bias before it poisons the corpus.
5. **🔴 Defer: shot-type / serve-type / per-shot labels.** Tempting (shot intelligence), but it multiplies cost and annotator skill needs, and it's future-track (the khelacharya layer), not v1 rally detection. Don't buy it yet.
6. **🔴 Avoid: anything player-identity / biometric** — GDPR Art. 9, and the footage contains minors. Keep it to rally/court/quality signal, never per-person tracking.

**Process levers the paid tier unlocks** (some already in the brief): the paid calibration round + joint review (§3 ✓), a real **escalation channel** for edge cases (§8 TODO — fill it), and asking them to **flag uncertainty rather than guess** (pairs with #2).

One honest caveat: every field added is more annotator time (→ higher per-rally price) **and** a schema change that ripples through `convert-cvat`'s attribute map, the canonical CSV, and the DB. So this is a genuine scope/cost call — which is why I'm asking rather than just rewriting it.

50	# The agreed cross-sport ending_reason vocabulary for net-separated sports.
51	# Kept here as a soft check only (a warning, never a hard failure) so this
52	# adapter does not depend on the annotation-spec wording.
53	DEFAULT_ALLOWED_REASONS = {
54	    "winner",
55	    "forced_error",
56	    "unforced_error",
57	    "service_fault",
58	    "let",
59	    "other",
60	}
61	
62	# The observational outcome of the last shot — orthogonal to ending_reason
63	# (winner can be 'in'; an error can be 'net' or 'out'; service_fault/let -> 'n_a').
64	DEFAULT_ALLOWED_OUTCOMES = {"in", "net", "out", "n_a"}
65	
66	# CVAT attribute names we map onto canonical columns. Vendor label schemas
67	# drift — suffixed names like "score_before (A-B)", and singular/plural or
68	# case/separator variants like "shot_count" (the Roora deliveries) for
69	# "shots_count" — so both these aliases and the incoming attribute names are
70	# folded through ``_fold_attr_name`` before matching.
71	_ATTR_ALIASES = {
72	    "rally_number": ("rally_number",),
73	    "shots_count": ("shots_count", "shot_count"),
74	    "ending_reason": ("ending_reason",),
75	    "last_shot_outcome": ("last_shot_outcome", "outcome", "last_shot_outcome (in/net/out)"),
76	    "score_before": ("score_before", "score_before (a-b)"),
77	    "score_after": ("score_after", "score_after (a-b)"),
78	    "notes": ("notes", "note"),
79	}
80	
81	
82	def _fold_attr_name(name: str) -> str:
83	    """Fold a CVAT attribute name to a tolerant matching key.
84	
85	    Case ("Shot_Count"), separators ("shot count", "shot-count"), parenthesized
86	    hints ("score_before (A-B)") and singular/plural ("shot_count" vs
87	    "shots_count") all fold to the same key, so a vendor's naming drift cannot
88	    silently drop a column.
89	    """
90	    s = re.sub(r"\(.*?\)", " ", (name or "").lower())
91	    tokens = [t for t in re.split(r"[\s_\-]+", s) if t]
92	    return "_".join(
93	        t[:-1] if len(t) > 1 and t.endswith("s") and not t.endswith("ss") else t
94	        for t in tokens)
95	
96	
97	_FOLDED_ALIASES = {canon: tuple(dict.fromkeys(_fold_attr_name(a) for a in aliases))
98	                   for canon, aliases in _ATTR_ALIASES.items()}
99	
100	CANONICAL_COLUMNS = [
101	    "rally_number", "start_mmss", "end_mmss", "start_time", "end_time",
102	    "shots_count", "ending_reason", "last_shot_outcome", "score_before", "score_after", "notes",
103	]

423	    base = boundaries[0]

441	
442	def convert(xml_path: str,
443	            fps: Optional[float] = None,
444	            video_path: Optional[str] = None,
445	            step: Optional[int] = None,
446	            allowed_reasons: Optional[set] = None) -> ConversionResult:
447	    """Full pipeline: parse CVAT XML -> records (+ issues) at the true fps.
448	
449	    ``fps`` resolution order: explicit ``fps`` arg > ffprobe of ``video_path`` >
450	    the CVAT meta (none) -> error. We never fall back to a guessed 30 fps, since
451	    that silent guess is exactly the bug this adapter exists to undo.
452	    """
453	    meta, boxes = parse_cvat_boxes(xml_path)
454	
455	    if fps is None and video_path:
456	        _, _, probed = probe_resolution_fps(video_path)
457	        fps = probed
458	    if fps is None or fps <= 0:
459	        raise ValueError(
460	            "Could not determine fps. Pass fps=... or a readable video_path so "
461	            "frames can be converted to seconds (seconds = frame / fps).")
[/external_agent_tool_result]

122	- A serve into the net is `service_fault` (rule 1 beats everything).
123	
124	### Then set `last_shot_outcome` — where the last shot physically went
125	This is a **separate, observational** column (no judgment): just look at the last shot.
126	- `in` — landed in (winners; also a put-away the opponent couldn't reach).
127	- `net` — went into the net.
128	- `out` — landed outside the court / past the lines.
129	- `n_a` — not applicable: use for `service_fault` and `let`.
130	
131	So each rally gets both: **why** (`ending_reason`) and **where** (`last_shot_outcome`) — e.g. a
132	smash winner = `winner, in`; a clear forced long = `forced_error, out`; a routine ball netted =
133	`unforced_error, net`.

227	- **Doubles?** Same rules — a shot is any one racket/paddle contact by whichever player hits it. See the badminton and pickleball **DOUBLES** notes in Part 5; never attribute `shots_count` to an individual, just total the contacts.
228	- **Server completely whiffs (no contact)?** Rare. True zero contact → `shots_count = 0`, `ending_reason = service_fault`, add a note. A graze = 1 contact = 1 shot.
229	
230	## Part 7 — Self-check before you submit (per video)
231	- [ ] `rally_number` is 1,2,3… no gaps or repeats.
232	- [ ] Every `start_time < end_time` (check the auto-filled decimal columns populated).
233	- [ ] No overlaps: `end_time[N] <= start_time[N+1]`.
234	- [ ] Times entered as `m:ss.mmm`; decimal `start_time`/`end_time` auto-filled and look right.
235	- [ ] No dead-time rows (scan the gaps — dead time, not missing rallies).
236	- [ ] Every served point present, including service faults and lets.
237	- [ ] `shots_count` looks right (re-step any rally you rushed; doubles = team total).
238	- [ ] `ending_reason` ∈ {`winner`,`forced_error`,`unforced_error`,`service_fault`,`let`,`other`}; notes filled when `other`.
239	- [ ] `last_shot_outcome` ∈ {`in`,`net`,`out`,`n_a`} (`n_a` for service_fault/let).
240	- [ ] File downloaded as `<video_id>.csv`, header row present, UTF-8.
241	- [ ] Did **not** trim / re-encode / rename the source video.
242	
243	## Part 8 — Mini glossary
244	- **Rally** — one continuous passage of play from serve contact to the shuttle/ball dead.
245	- **Shot** — one contact between the shuttle/ball and a racket/paddle/bat (either teammate in doubles).
246	- **Dead time** — any time the shuttle/ball is not in active play. Never labeled.
247	- **Service fault** — a failed/illegal serve that ends the point. Still a labeled rally.
248	- **Let** — a replayed point. Labeled as its own rally with `ending_reason=let`.
249	- **Winner / forced_error / unforced_error** — the final shot was a clean winner (opponent made no legal contact) / the loser's error was forced by a pressuring shot / the loser's error was on a routine, unpressured ball.
250	- **start_time / end_time** — decimal seconds from the start of the provided file (auto-filled from mm:ss).
251

86	step-sampled job that assumes 30 fps writes systematically wrong times. fps comes
87	from `--fps` or ffprobe of `--video-path`. `mm:ss` is also accepted everywhere.
88	
89	### What does validation check?
90	| code | severity | meaning |
91	|---|---|---|
92	| `overlap` | **blocker** | two rallies' intervals overlap (`end[N] > start[N+1]`) |
93	| `zero_duration` | **blocker** | `end <= start` (single-sample / stray box) |
94	| `boundary_grid_snap` | **blocker** | every rally boundary sits on one frame-sampling grid coarser than the ±0.3s bar — first/last boxes were snapped to sampled frames instead of the exact contact/dead frame (the pickleball/TT pilot defect) |
95	| `frame_out_of_range` | high | a box frame is past the video's last frame |
96	| `shot_density` | medium | long rally with implausibly few shots → likely an **inflated span** (often hides a merged rally) |
97	| `possible_missed_rally` | medium | a long unlabeled gap between two rallies → check for an **omitted rally** |
98	| `ending_reason_vocab` | medium | `ending_reason` outside the agreed set |
99	| `outcome_vocab` | low | `last_shot_outcome` outside `{in,net,out,n_a}` |
100	
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## What

Now that the Roora program is paid, ask for the signals that matter most — and keep **what we ask** (guidelines) and **what we capture** (the `convert-cvat` parser) in one atomic change so they can''t drift.

**Per-rally additions** (Brief schema + Guide how-to + ingestion):
- **`court` (`primary`|`secondary`)** for **multi-court** videos — the gold label for the model''s #1 weakness (background-court pickups; 0.66 vs 0.93 clean). Carried `RallyRecord → canonical CSV → round-trip`, case-normalised, soft `court_vocab` check. Tagged on multi-court videos, blank on single-court.
- **`confidence` (`sure`|`unsure`)** — an honest-doubt flag; `unsure` routes to the review sheet via a new low-severity `annotator_unsure` issue (never penalised, never a blocker); soft `confidence_vocab` check.

**Process / doc additions:**
- **Per-video capture report** (`court_layout` / `full_court_in_frame` / `occlusion` / `lighting` / `camera_motion` / `comment`) — feeds our recording guidelines; doc-only.
- **Inter-annotator (A/A) overlap ~15% sample** in the QA section + a pricing line — leverages the existing metrics IAA capability (your A/A idea, now affordable).

## Scope note
`court`/`confidence` live in the **canonical CSV + review sheet** today; DB/export integration (per-sport schema columns) is a tracked follow-up **before we train on them** — `import-local` still reads the core columns only. This keeps the urgent guidelines change atomic and small.

## Verification
Suite **205 green**, ruff clean. New tests: court/confidence parse + case-normalise, canonical-CSV round-trip, `unsure`→review (not error), off-vocab warnings. `INGESTION_PIPELINE` validation table + canonical-CSV definition updated.

Two new **per-rally** columns + two **process** additions, all wired so the parser captures them (no "ask-then-drop"):

## Paste-ready Doc text

**① Brief — add these two rows to the schema table (after `last_shot_outcome`):**
```
| court | multi-court videos only: primary (the main court the camera is filming) | secondary (any background court). Leave blank for single-court videos. We tell you which videos are multi-court in the intake manifest. |
| confidence | optional: unsure if you want us to double-check this rally (boundary, count, or call you weren't certain of); blank or sure otherwise. Flagging honest doubt is encouraged, not penalised. |
```

**② Brief — replace the example CSV header+rows with:**
```
rally_number,start_mmss,end_mmss,start_time,end_time,shots_count,ending_reason,last_shot_outcome,court,confidence,score_before,score_after,notes
1,0:12.50,0:25.00,12.50,25.00,9,winner,in,primary,,0-0,1-0,
2,0:45.00,0:47.30,45.00,47.30,1,service_fault,n_a,primary,,1-0,1-1,serve into net
3,1:01.20,1:18.85,61.20,78.85,14,unforced_error,out,secondary,unsure,,,background court — partly occluded
```

**③ Brief — new section "Per-video capture report" (one short note per video):**
```
court_layout: single | multi
full_court_in_frame: yes | no (if no, what's cut off)
occlusion: none | brief description (net post, player, scoreboard, people crossing)
lighting: ok | glare | dark
camera_motion: static | shaky | panned (the tool needs a STATIC camera)
comment: anything that made rallies hard to read
```

**④ Brief — add to QA + pricing:** an inter-annotator **A/A overlap (~15% of videos, two independent annotators on the same file)** as an objective quality floor, priced as a separate line; plus a pricing line for the `court`/`confidence` add-ons.

**⑤ Guide — new "Court & confidence" how-to:** `primary` = the in-focus front court that fills the frame; `secondary` = any other court visible; label every clearly-visible rally and tag its court; blank on single-court. `confidence = unsure` for any rally you're not certain about — it routes to our review, never counts against you.

(All of this is already live in `ROORA_BRIEF.md` + `ANNOTATION_GUIDE.md` on master, so if you'd rather, just regenerate the Doc from the repo instead of pasting.)

## My feedback on getting richer info now that we pay

The **court tag is the highest-leverage thing in this whole change** — it directly attacks the one place the model is weak (multi-court, 0.66 vs 0.93), and you can't buy that signal any other way. The **A/A overlap** turns your own duplicate-test instinct into a standing quality metric. Two honest caveats:

1. **One scope boundary I drew deliberately:** `court`/`confidence` currently flow into the canonical CSV + review sheet but **not yet into the DB/training export** — so to actually *train* on court tags we need a follow-up PR (per-sport schema columns + migration). I kept that out of this change so the urgent guidelines could ship atomically; it's queued and noted.
2. **What I deliberately did *not* add:** per-shot/shot-type labels (multiplies cost; that's the khelacharya shot-intelligence track, not v1) and anything player-identity/biometric (GDPR Art. 9 + minors in footage). Worth revisiting shot-type only once rally detection is locked.

When you're back from lunch: the batch summary should be ready, and the only manual bits left on the Doc are the `TODO(avidullu)` placeholders (Drive links, template Sheet, escalation channel) plus pasting the blocks above.

## One thing to decide before sharing the link

**Total is 41.3 GB.** Google Drive now has to upload all of that to the cloud before your share link is usable — that's a long upload on a typical connection, and a big download for Roora. Two paths:

1. **Ship as-is** — let Drive finish uploading the 41 GB; share the folder link once it syncs. Simplest, best quality.

My lean: **wait and see the actual Drive upload speed first** — if it's syncing fine, ship as-is (path 1); only re-run if the upload is painfully slow. Check Google Drive's sync status (the system-tray icon) before you send the link.

}

### 994. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.147Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: GPU NVENC 1080p downscale of the 5 Boxhill matches, original names, to Drive
command: $root="F:\GoPro Hero Black 12\KhelSutra\Badminton 12 June\100GOPRO\Badminton"
$out="G:\My Drive\Sharing\Badminton\Boxhill\June 12 2026"
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $out | Out-Null
$exts=@(".mp4",".mov",".mkv",".avi",".m4v")
$vids = Get-ChildItem $root -File | Where-Object { $exts -contains $_.Extension.ToLower() } | Sort-Object Name
$n=0; $ok=0; $skip=0; $fail=@()
foreach($v in $vids){
  $n++
  $dst = Join-Path $out $v.Name
  if(Test-Path $dst){ "[$n/$($vids.Count)] SKIP (exists): $($v.Name)"; $skip++; continue }
  "[$n/$($vids.Count)] ENCODING: $($v.Name)  $(Get-Date -Format HH:mm:ss)"
  $tmp = "$dst.partial.mp4"
  ffmpeg -hide_banner -loglevel warning -stats -y -i $v.FullName -map 0:V:0 -map "0:a?" -vf "scale=-2:2*trunc(min(1080\,ih)/2)" -c:v h264_nvenc -preset p5 -rc vbr -cq 23 -b:v 0 -pix_fmt yuv420p -c:a aac -b:a 160k -movflags +faststart $tmp
  if($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0 -and (Test-Path $tmp)){ Move-Item -Force $tmp $dst; $ok++; "  DONE: $([int]((Get-Item $dst).Length/1MB)) MB" }
  else{ if(Test-Path $tmp){Remove-Item -Force $tmp}; $fail+=$v.Name; "  FAILED: $($v.Name)" }
}
"=== SUMMARY: encoded=$ok skipped=$skip failed=$($fail.Count) ==="
if($fail){"Failed: $($fail -join ', ')"}
"Output: $out" | Out-File -Append "$out\_encode_done.txt"
[/external_agent_tool_call]

Easy fix: drop the `/2` (these are all 4K→1080, so `min(1080,ih)` needs no even-rounding) and avoid `Remove-Item` (leave failed `.partial` files instead of deleting, `-y` overwrites on retry). ### 997. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.147Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: GPU NVENC 1080p downscale of 5 Boxhill matches to Drive (sandbox-safe)
command: $root="F:\GoPro Hero Black 12\KhelSutra\Badminton 12 June\100GOPRO\Badminton"
$out="G:\My Drive\Sharing\Badminton\Boxhill\June 12 2026"
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $out | Out-Null
$vf = "scale=-2:min(1080\,ih)"
$exts=@(".mp4",".mov",".mkv",".avi",".m4v")
$vids = Get-ChildItem $root -File | Where-Object { $exts -contains $_.Extension.ToLower() } | Sort-Object Name
$n=0; $ok=0; $skip=0; $fail=@()
foreach($v in $vids){
  $n++
  $dst = Join-Path $out $v.Name
  if(Test-Path $dst){ "[$n/$($vids.Count)] SKIP (exists): $($v.Name)"; $skip++; continue }
  "[$n/$($vids.Count)] ENCODING: $($v.Name)  $(Get-Date -Format HH:mm:ss)"
  $tmp = "$dst.partial.mp4"
  ffmpeg -hide_banner -loglevel warning -stats -y -i $v.FullName -map 0:V:0 -map "0:a?" -vf $vf -c:v h264_nvenc -preset p5 -rc vbr -cq 23 -b:v 0 -pix_fmt yuv420p -c:a aac -b:a 160k -movflags +faststart $tmp
  if($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0 -and (Test-Path $tmp)){ Move-Item -Force $tmp $dst; $ok++; "  DONE: $([int]((Get-Item $dst).Length/1MB)) MB" }
  else{ $fail+=$v.Name; "  FAILED (left .partial for retry): $($v.Name)" }
}
"=== SUMMARY: encoded=$ok skipped=$skip failed=$($fail.Count) ==="
if($fail){"Failed: $($fail -join ', ')"}
